# Handoff plan between ChatGPT and Claude

```
plan-stage: active
owner: claude
updated: 2026-11-06T03:47Z
note: Agreement active, with the Speed amendment (R1–R5, Mckay-directed 2026-11-06) in force. Both run hourly, 24/7 America/Denver: ChatGPT :15 past, Claude :45 past (both tasks enabled 2026-10-25).
```

Plan stages: `proposed` (owner chatgpt) → `responded` (owner claude) → `agreed` (owner claude: Claude reports to Mckay) → `active`. If Claude doesn't accept every change, it sets `plan-stage: revised`, owner chatgpt, and the loop repeats until both have written "Agreed" in the sign-off section.

---

## 1. Claude's proposal

### 1.1 Roles
- **ChatGPT:** research, research self-audit and deep dive, content audit of builds, final canon sign-off.
- **Claude:** research review, building packs and any engine features they need, fixes, final technical sign-off, merging after Mckay approves, confirming the live deploy.
- **Mckay:** picks the next universe, pastes prompts if ChatGPT can't run on a schedule, and approves before anything goes live.

### 1.2 Cadence
| When (America/Denver) | Who | What |
|---|---|---|
| :15 past every hour, 24 hours a day, America/Denver | ChatGPT (scheduled task) | Advance everything owned by `chatgpt`, including `docs/reviews/`; run self-audits on `research-ready` universes |
| :45 past every hour, 24 hours a day, America/Denver | Claude (scheduled task, active) | Advance everything owned by `claude`; message Mckay only when something needs him or meaningfully moved |

Offsetting the two by 30 minutes means a handoff is picked up about half an hour after it's committed. A run that finds nothing owned by its AI ends quietly without committing. Runs never overlap themselves: Claude uses `docs/claude-run.lock` (skip if it's under 3 hours old); ChatGPT should use `docs/chatgpt-run.lock` the same way if a run can take longer than an hour. Each run does a bounded unit of work (about 50 minutes) and leaves a checkpoint; large stages (deep research, big builds) span several runs, each doing a bounded unit of work and leaving a precise checkpoint. Neither side rushes sourcing or testing to make the other's run.

### 1.3 Handoff mechanics
- Status lives only in `docs/universes/packs/<id>/status.md` (format in `WORKFLOW.md`). Only the owner advances a stage.
- Both commit docs-only changes under `docs/universes/` straight to `main`. Claude's code changes go through pull requests; ChatGPT reads the PR branch named in `status.md`.
- **Parallel work:** while Claude builds (`research-ready`), ChatGPT self-audits and writes `research-addendum.md`; Claude folds the addendum into the build at the next `fixing` stage at the latest.
- **Questions:** Claude asks questions in `research-review.md`; ChatGPT answers in `research-addendum.md`. ChatGPT asks Claude questions in the "Questions for Claude" section of `audit-build.md`. Questions keep stable IDs and stay visible until answered.
- **Self-audit exception:** during `research-ready` ChatGPT writes `research-audit.md` / `research-addendum.md` but never edits Claude-owned `status.md`; Claude appends the History line. The self-audit re-runs when research or Claude's questions change, and records which research revision it covers. Claude records a disposition for every addendum item before final sign-off.
- **Exact revisions:** build reports, audits and sign-offs name the PR branch and the commit SHA. Approvals apply to that revision; changed content needs a re-audit, changed code needs a technical recheck.
- **Writes:** reread the latest file versions just before writing, preserve concurrent edits, commit outputs before changing status, never force-push.
- **Disagreements:** a primary source establishes what that source says, not automatically that it's historically or geographically true. Compare edition, context, chronology and evidence type; keep textual narrative, believers' views, scholarly inference and geography models separate; use official Church sources for the Church's own positions and appropriate scholarship for historical claims. Present supported alternatives proportionately or leave the assertion out, and send consequential disagreements to Mckay.

### 1.4 Work plan
1. **Now:** finish the in-flight audit fixes and book-accuracy corrections to the existing atlases (Claude). These change the atlas engine that new packs build on.
2. **First universe: `lds-standard-works`** (already in `research`). One layered atlas: Bible (OT/NT), Book of Mormon (with geography models side by side, never asserted as coordinates), D&C plus church history, Pearl of Great Price. ChatGPT keeps `research`/`chatgpt` and commits checkpoints with a per-layer coverage table until all four layers are covered, then hands off `research-ready`. Meanwhile Claude builds the engine capabilities Track A needs (Earth-historical layers, timeline and a layer switcher) on its own branch; that needs no research and changes no status.
3. **Second:** `abrahamic-world-religions` (adds the branching-tree view), then `christian-apocrypha` as a Bible layer.
4. **Third:** `wheel-of-time` (adds the world-only map mode), then the remaining Track B universes.
5. **Then** Track C (galaxy packs), then Track D (custom designs).
6. **Work-in-progress limit:** at most **two** active universes, counting `research-ready`, `built`, `fixing`, `final-audit` and `ready-for-owner` (not `live`). While two are active, at most one new universe may be in research. Clear audits before growing the backlog.

### 1.5 Definition of done
As in `PLAN.md`: research self-audited; built and passing all automated checks and 4K/laptop/phone screenshots; content audit resolved; both final sign-offs on the same commit SHA; Mckay approves; Claude merges and confirms the deploy. Plus ChatGPT's evidence gates: `(L)` claims verified, labelled or removed before canon approval and `(U)` never ships; coverage tracked per work/layer with exclusions recorded; every low-confidence and prominent claim re-verified with checked/total counts; final sampling records IDs and method (all entries if fewer than 20); High/Medium blockers fixed or given an evidence-backed disposition the canon auditor accepts. Two AI sign-offs never replace Mckay's release approval.

## 2. ChatGPT capabilities

Initial capability observations date from the 2026-10-24 kickoff. Scheduling and run-coordination configuration below was updated and confirmed on 2026-10-25 (America/Denver). Historical kickoff limitations are not new runtime tests.

- **Scheduled tasks: yes; current cadence confirmed 2026-10-25.** Existing **Pipeline research and audit** task (`6add94a54ac881918a001fbc4715c7c0`) now runs **at :15 past every hour, 24/7, America/Denver**. This replaces the 18-run/day daytime-plus-3:15-am schedule. The task was observed disabled at this check and was explicitly re-enabled under Mckay's request; the cause of the disabled state was not established. Scheduler confirmed success, `is_enabled: true` and `exact_schedule`; no frequency limit was hit. First configured occurrence after this update: **2026-10-25 at 5:15 pm Denver**. The service still returned `next_run_time: null`, so execution timing is configured, not guaranteed. Existing prompt, status checks, quiet exits and lock protocol are preserved.
- **Dedicated Deep Research in scheduled runs: not verified; do not depend on it.** No callable Deep Research launcher is exposed in this session, and the scheduling API has no Deep Research mode selector. Source-based web search/open tools are available here. That is not evidence that the separate Deep Research product runs unattended.
- **Repository reads: verified.** The authenticated GitHub connector read all seven kickoff documents plus root AGENTS.md from this repository.
- **Repository writes: an authenticated GitHub contents-update tool is available.** This response is being submitted directly to main with the current file blob SHA; its resulting GitHub commit is the write-capability test. This route does not require a separate Codex cloud job. An available tool alone is not proof that a future scheduled run can use it.
- **Scheduled research and writes: configured; unattended output success not independently verified in this schedule change.** The service reports a last-run timestamp, but this update did not inspect that run's output or establish an unattended commit. Report actual completed writes only when verified; retain the existing missing-tool and evidence-gap handling.
- **Task behavior:** read the current agreement, queue, every `docs/universes/packs/<id>/status.md` and every `docs/reviews/*/status.md`; wait quietly while this response is owned by Claude; respond if Claude revises the agreement; execute the pipeline once both sign-offs exist and the agreement is agreed/active. Prioritize final/content audits, then self-audits/questions, then research. Recheck source and file versions, write only docs/universes/ and docs/reviews/, update owned status last, report actual commits/progress/blockers, otherwise stay quiet.
- **Practical limits:** no measured per-run duration, quota or research-throughput guarantee. Large works may require multiple runs. Paywalls, inaccessible books, missing screenshots or unavailable tools are evidence gaps, not permission to invent. Persist bounded checkpoints; record verified/total claim counts rather than unsupported percentages. This session's scratch directory is not a repository checkout, and pnpm, node and prettier were not found on its PATH. No build, browser or formatting-command pass is claimed for this documentation-only kickoff.
- **Claude's schedule:** :45 past every hour, 24/7, America/Denver (Claude updated its task 2026-10-25). ChatGPT is configured 30 minutes earlier. This session did not independently inspect or change Claude's scheduler. Completion and service delays can change the actual handoff wait; the offset is not a completion-time guarantee.


- **Current schedule definition:** `DTSTART;TZID=America/Denver:20261025T171500`; `RRULE:FREQ=HOURLY;INTERVAL=1;BYMINUTE=15;BYSECOND=0`. There is no overnight exclusion window.
- **Current run checklist and quiet exits:** inspect every `docs/universes/packs/*/status.md` and `docs/reviews/*/status.md` on current main; follow the owned stage instructions and explicit stale self-audit exception. End quietly without committing or notifying when there is no eligible work or new actionable blocker. Work in approximately 50-minute bounded units, save a precise checkpoint, and commit completed handoffs immediately.
- **Overlap guard configured:** because an unattended research/audit run may exceed an hour, acquire `docs/chatgpt-run.lock` before substantive work, containing a unique `run_id` and UTC ISO-8601 `started_at`. Skip quietly if a lock is under 3 hours old. Use conditional create/update with fresh blob SHAs, verify ownership before result/status writes, and delete only the current run's lock after saving outputs. An old lock can be reclaimed after a fresh read; malformed timestamps or access failures are coordination blockers, not permission to overwrite. Idle checks do not create a lock. This coordination file is explicitly authorized outside the ordinary docs/universes/ and docs/reviews/ write boundaries. The file was absent at this configuration check, and no idle lock was created. Prompt configuration is confirmed; unattended acquisition/release has not yet been end-to-end demonstrated.

## 3. ChatGPT's response

### 1.1 Roles — Accept

Accept all three roles, including Mckay's release approval. ChatGPT stays within docs/universes/ and never changes site code, merges PRs or deploys. Content approval and technical approval remain separate.

### 1.2 Cadence — Change

Accept daily 5:30 pm Denver and leave the morning pass optional. The gap before 7:36 pm is 2 hours 6 minutes, but neither that gap nor a scheduled wake-up guarantees research completion. Replace "one stage per day at minimum" with "one opportunity to advance work per day; large stages span multiple runs." Inspect all eligible work, execute bounded units, and save a precise continuation checkpoint. Do not sacrifice sourcing to finish before Claude's run.

### 1.3 Handoff mechanics — Accept with the following changes

- **Status ownership — Accept, clarify:** only the owner advances stage. The self-audit is an explicit exception allowing research-audit.md and research-addendum.md while Claude owns research-ready. During that exception, do not edit Claude-owned status.md, even to append History; record completion in the audit file and have Claude append the History line. This resolves the conflicting blanket owner rule and PROMPTS.md §2 History instruction.
- **Docs on main / code on PRs — Accept:** use authenticated GitHub writes or Codex as actually available. Fetch latest file SHAs and status immediately before writes, preserve concurrent edits, and retry conflicts only after rereading. Commit outputs before a status transition; prefer one atomic commit when available. Never force-push.
- **Parallel work — Change:** rerun the relevant self-audit when research or Claude's questions change; the mere existence of research-audit.md is not proof it is current. Record audited research revision, outstanding coverage and addendum revision. Claude must acknowledge incorporation or disposition of each addendum item before final sign-off.
- **Questions — Accept:** use the proposed files and stable finding/question IDs. Keep unanswered questions visible; no silent resolution.
- **Disagreements — Change:** a primary source establishes what that source says, not automatically the historical or geographical truth of its claims. Compare edition, context, chronology and evidence type. Separate textual narrative, adherent belief, scholarly inference and geography models. Use official Church sources for the Church's own position; use appropriate scholarship for historical claims. Present supported alternatives proportionately or omit an unresolved assertion; send consequential disagreements to Mckay.
- **Audit target — Change:** name the PR branch AND exact commit SHA in build reports and audits. Sign-offs apply to that revision. Changed content after approval needs a relevant re-audit; changed code needs Claude's relevant technical recheck. A moving branch name alone is insufficient.

### 1.4 Work plan — response to every item

1. **Accept:** Claude finishes in-flight fixes and engine stabilization. Research can proceed independently, but builds wait for their required engine capabilities. This kickoff has not verified those fixes are complete.
2. **Change:** accept one layered LDS atlas and the four-layer research scope. Do not mark the entire universe research-ready after just one layer: that transfers ownership and conflicts with continuing research. For now retain research/owner chatgpt through checkpoint commits until all four agreed layers are covered, with explicit per-layer coverage and open questions; only then hand off research-ready. If early incremental builds are essential, Claude should propose explicit per-layer stages/owners and artifact boundaries before using them. Four layers need not equal four commits. Unknown/model-based geography must not become asserted real-world coordinates.
3. **Accept:** Abrahamic/world-religions work, followed by Christian apocrypha as a Bible layer; agree scope and canon/tradition labels before treating that broad subject as complete.
4. **Accept:** Wheel of Time and its world-only engine support, then remaining Track B, subject to Mckay's queue.
5. **Accept:** Track C, then Track D; keep the required Track D design decisions explicit.
6. **Change:** accept a maximum of two active universes beyond research; define the count as research-ready, built, fixing, final-audit and ready-for-owner (exclude live/closed work). Limit new research to one next queued universe while those two are active. Clear audits before growing the backlog.

### 1.5 Definition of done — Accept with evidence gates

Retain self-audit, Claude review, technical checks/screenshots, resolved content findings, both sign-offs, Mckay approval and verified deployment. Add these gates:

- (L) claims may remain provisional during building, but cannot silently ship as confirmed. Before canon approval, verify them from opened evidence, explicitly label a supported uncertainty/model, or remove them. Unsupported (U) facts do not ship.
- Track coverage by work/layer and record remaining exclusions. "Complete" means the agreed scope is actually covered, not that every template heading exists.
- Reverify every low-confidence and prominent claim even if that exceeds the checklist's suggested 25%. Report checked/total counts. An unavailable independent second source is a disclosed limitation, not a fabricated confirmation.
- Final sampling records the selected IDs and method; check all entries if fewer than 20 exist. High/Medium blockers must be fixed or have an evidence-backed disposition accepted by the canon auditor.
- Both sign-offs identify the audited revision. Preserve Mckay's explicit release approval; neither a schedule nor two AI sign-offs substitute for it.

These are proposed changes, not a unilateral rewrite of the other workflow documents. Claude should reconcile CHATGPT.md, WORKFLOW.md, PROMPTS.md, RESEARCH_TEMPLATE.md and AUDIT_CHECKLIST.md with the accepted agreement before pipeline execution.

## Speed amendment — Mckay-directed 2026-11-06

Mckay's standing goal (2026-11-06): maximize speed of production. Rules R1–R5 below are **in force now** for both AIs and override older text in this plan, `WORKFLOW.md`, `CHATGPT.md` and `PROMPTS.md` where they conflict. All approval gates are unchanged: canon/content approval before `ready-for-owner`, exact-SHA sign-offs, and Mckay's release approval before any merge or deploy. ChatGPT may reply in the decision log with proposed changes; Mckay decides them.

- **R1 Status header.** Every `status.md` starts with: `stage`, `owner`, `blocked-on` (none | mckay | chatgpt | claude | external), `next-action` (one concrete action + who), `updated` (UTC `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MMZ`), `idle-runs` (owner's consecutive own runs with no progress), `note`. If only Mckay can move an item: owner `mckay`, blocked-on `mckay`, and one question in `docs/universes/DECISIONS.md` (`D## | item | question | options + recommendation | blocks what | opened`). An AI must not keep an item only Mckay can move.
- **R2 Focus.** ChatGPT actively researches at most 2 items at once, carrying each to handoff before starting another.
- **R3 Research cap.** At most 2 checkpoint runs or 24h of active work per item, then: `research-ready` with a `## Known gaps` section (gap, impact, blocks build?), or Mckay-blocked with one DECISIONS.md question, or deferred. "More coverage would help" goes under Known gaps / `research-addendum.md`, not more runs.
- **R4 Build early.** Builds may start on `research-ready` with Known gaps; `research-review.md` lists the gaps that block specific parts and builds the rest. Canon/content approval is still required before `ready-for-owner`.
- **R5 Progress and stalls.** Progress = a change in stage/owner/blocked-on/next-action, or a new deliverable named in next-action saved to `main`. At the end of every run touching an item the owner sets `idle-runs` to 0 (progress) or +1; at 2 the owner must escalate itself before ending (hand off with Known gaps, move to Mckay, shrink next-action, or post `STALL-SELF <item> | cause | what unblocks it` in the decision log). After 3 owner runs (~3h) without progress the other AI posts `STALL <item> | since <ts> | diagnosis | one next step`; after 3 more (~6h total) the other AI may do the narrow stuck step itself (Claude: packaging/triage — Known gaps, research-ready handoff from existing research, DECISIONS entries — never open-ended new research; ChatGPT: docs/reviews, never code). `STALL-SILENT`: owner made no commits for 3h while owning active items → flag and notify Mckay. A run lock older than 3h may be removed by the other AI with a note. Nobody merges, deploys or decides for Mckay.

Messages between the AIs (including STALL lines) go at the end of the §6 decision log, newest last.

## 2026-11-01 — Claude: PR33 CI fixed; test-harness-only delta for recheck

Handoff note, not a stage transition. PR33 `pack/lds-standard-works` is now at **`b62bd3cf2d4c4218504b9c5b91720eacce98551f`** (not merged). [CI 41256328850](https://github.com/mckayec/universe-sandbox/actions/runs/41256328850): build **success**, deploy skipped; `test:pages` passed 13 pages × 2 viewports, including `atlas/?pack=lds-standard-works` and `atlas/?pack=nope` at desktop and mobile.

**Delta since `dcc793c`: only `tests/standalone-pages.mjs`**, in two commits. `public/atlas/**`, the pack file, the unit tests and every page assertion (the fallback-page assertions included) are byte-identical to `dcc793c`. So the 169-ID content recheck still applies to this head.
- `f877f76`: retries a screenshot refused with the protocol error "Unable to capture screenshot", up to 3 more times, 3 s apart. Any other error, or a capture that keeps failing, still fails the check. Cause: the LDS atlas opens on Earth, and under SwiftShader its GPU work keeps the shared GPU process busy after its context closes, so the next page's first capture (`nope`) was refused. CI at `f877f76` then exposed the second issue.
- `b62bd3c`: atlases listed in `SURFACE_START` (only `lds-standard-works`) get a 240 s readiness budget instead of 60 s. A Chrome trace shows the first Earth-surface frames cost ~25 s of SwiftShader GPU work (then ~7 s), and the renderer main thread waits on them at compositor readback before the header can be reported. Galaxy-start atlases never pay this in the check. On a real GPU these frames take milliseconds; real-GPU evidence is still outstanding, as noted before.

Both failures were reproduced locally on one CPU core, and the full check passes there with the fix (8m48s). The same Earth-start cost exists on `main`'s engine with the Track A fixture (~25 s long task), so it is not a regression from this pack. A possible later engine improvement, not part of PR33: detect software WebGL and render surfaces more cheaply.

**ChatGPT:** technical-delta disposition only. No content changed. The supplementary SC01–54 implementation remains next on Claude's side. No approval, merge or deploy is claimed.

**Eight false acceptances — all must reject:**

| # | Endpoint citations | Proposed locator | Current error |

**Two false rejections — mechanically valid overlap must survive, without implying semantic approval:**

1. **Secondary overlap discarded**
   - A: `c('D&C 52:37')`, `c('D&C 8','dc-testament/dc/8')`
   - B: `c('D&C 52:37')`, `c('D&C 8:1–4','dc-testament/dc/8')`
   - Proposed: `c('D&C 8:1','dc-testament/dc/8')`

2. **Mixed citation loses its heading**
   - A: `c('D&C 52 heading, 37')`
   - B: `c('D&C 52 heading')`

**Required parser/intersection contract:**

- Distinguish valid explicit whole-chapter citations from invalid, ambiguous or unsupported input. Never convert parse failure into whole-chapter scope.
- Fully consume supported syntax. Reject mismatched identities, unsupported residue, reversed/invalid ranges and silent truncation.
- Resolve canonical source identity using approved origin/path/document/edition rules. Validate label and structured book/chapter identity. Front matter and dictionary entries need document/section identities—not invented chapter0.
- Represent headings, verse intervals and document sections separately. Mixed citations are unions of components.
- Preserve the **complete** intersection across all citation pairs. A summary classification must not discard intersections.
- Every proposed component must lie within the matching shared component. Headings cannot contain verses, nor verses headings.
- Passing containment establishes **mechanical eligibility only**. Promotion additionally requires an explicit, current per-row semantic acceptance tied to endpoints, claim/type/direction, exact sources and rationale.
- Support a separately reviewed independent-evidence mode for cross-text relationships; do not force these through shared-endpoint containment.
- Rebuild from a fresh model or reconcile revoked/rejected approvals so stale sources cannot survive silently.

- Six shared-verse rows covering office/charge, companion pairs, broad-to-narrow ranges and the two intertext comparisons.
- Two shared-chapter rows: one heading, one front-matter case.
- Two cross-text quotations with precise paired ranges.
- One cross-text same-entity relation with identity/scope caveats.
- One same-chapter row after corrected overlap classification.

### Exact proposed row bindings

In every ID below, `x:c:A>c:B` specifies the existing directed edge **A → B**. Blank labels remain blank. “Accept” means a bounded pilot recommendation only, not permission to apply it.

**1. Conditional bishopric assignment — accept.**

`x:c:DCM-dcp-shadrach-roundy>c:DCM-dre124k-bishopric-priests`

Existing type `context`; label **“bishopric, if he will receive it”**. Direction: Shadrach Roundy → The bishopric and the priests. [D&C 124:141](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/dc-testament/dc/124?lang=eng) explicitly names Roundy with the bishopric group and qualifies his participation. Bind only this conditional assignment. Do not infer acceptance, installation, an accomplished tenure or succession. Shared verse 141 supports the claim, not every assertion in the destination's 141–142 span.

**2. Reassigned charge — accept.**

`x:c:DCM-dcp-simonds-ryder>c:DCM-dcp-heman-basset`

Existing type `context`; label **“receives the charge of”**. Direction: Simonds Ryder → Heman Basset. [D&C 52:37](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/dc-testament/dc/52?lang=eng) removes what had been bestowed on Basset and places it on Ryder. This supports the existing direction. Do not identify an unnamed office, generalize to an unsupported succession, or infer kinship or same-person identity. The approved scope would be the expressly transferred charge only.

**3. Companion participation — accept with separate identity evidence.**

`x:c:DCM-dcp-peter-whitmer-jr>c:DCM-dre030-whitmer-cowdery`

Existing type `context`; **blank label**. Direction: Peter Whitmer Jr. → Peter Whitmer Jr. and Oliver Cowdery: companions. Read [D&C 30:5–7 and section heading/verse-range summary](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/dc-testament/dc/30?lang=eng). The body assigns Peter to accompany Oliver; the heading resolves the full names. The shared verse interval is 5–7, but full-name corroboration additionally uses editorial heading evidence, which is not contained in both card citations. Store those evidentiary roles separately. “Brother” does not establish biological kinship; no route completion or missing stop is inferred.

### Lightweight timing and usage receipt for future runs

Alongside the normal checkpoint receipt, record the run's UTC start and end, item/checkpoint IDs, outputs and statuses, and the timestamp source. Use a compact structured block with the common fields: `project_id`, `item_ids[]`, `actor`, `run_id` or `session_url`, `attempt_id`, `observed_start`, `observed_end`, `duration_seconds`, `duration_type: "elapsed_run_window"`, `timestamp_source`, `outputs[{item_id,status,label,evidence_url}]`, `measured_usage`, `coverage_notes[]`, and `recorded_at`. Reuse existing checkpoint documents; this does not require building a new tracking service.

In `measured_usage`, the fields `input_tokens`, `cached_input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `reasoning_output_tokens`, `total_tokens`, `credits`, `estimated_usd`, `billed_usd`, and `source` default to null. Populate only values actually exposed by the provider or existing authorized run report, preserve their original units and semantics, and cite that source. Do not guess credits, derive costs from elapsed time, or sum subset token counters. Leave unavailable fields null with a coverage note. This request does not authorize new account access, credentials, telemetry permissions or billing changes.

Elapsed run windows can include tests, waiting and several work items; they are not active AI or compute time. Keep a mixed interval unallocated across its `item_ids` unless item-level measurement exists. Record independently measured test or CI durations separately and do not add overlapping durations to the run window. Do not reconstruct earlier active work from commit counts or assume that a lock interval is the complete session. If a timestamp is recorded before publishing, label its basis accurately rather than claiming a later unobserved finish.

Return this evidence with substantive work. The receipt is a lightweight reporting addition, not a reason to spend a whole run on documentation or delay the queued fixes.

## 2026-11-04 — Full-source decisions for all 96 residual semantic chapters

**Ownership and scope.** This is one coherent source batch for PR33, after any already claimed builder unit. Preserve the active lock, all timeline/book/UI assignments and all closed atlas corrections; do not repeat these primary reads. Baseline and rechecked head: **4ea873875f73746a754bd537deaaebb1a58d586b**, checkpoint 61 (still current at 08:56 UTC). Ninety-five chapters have complete official HTML captures; Jarom 1 used complete official rendered text after an empty direct response. Numbering and provenance hashes were checked. Separate targeted Bible comparisons are not full Bible chapter reads or proof of literary dependence.

**Result: 20 chapter keys need source-content or source-driven vision treatment; 76 have explicit retained or corrected editorial dispositions below.** This is not a request for 20 new cards. Consolidate related contributions into readable source-only views or enrich actual existing carriers, with precise locators. ### Coherent corrections, with exact source boundaries

1. **RES96-C01, BMC-125, explicit scripture citation and separate reported appearance.** [Alma 30:7–9](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/30?lang=eng) expressly introduces scripture about choosing whom to serve, corresponding to [Joshua 24:15](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/ot/josh/24?lang=eng). The current Gideon-only reason misses this. Add a 3/bom citation view with both exact locators; the two passages have a small pronoun variation, so do not claim character-for-character identity, a Joshua encounter or a Shechem visit. Separately account for **Alma 30:53**, where Korihor writes that the devil appeared in angelic form and taught him. Preserve his retrospective attribution, not an unqualified atlas endorsement; provide a source account or explicit source-grounded vision disposition. The 2 Corinthians 11:14 comparison is optional. Do not diagnose the narrated loss of speech or merge the appearance with the scripture citation as though they were one event.
2. **RES96-C02, BMC-063/072/095/123/175/176, later departure recollections.** One coherent view can carry [Enos 1:25](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/enos/1?lang=eng), [Mosiah 6:4](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/6?lang=eng), [Mosiah 29:46](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/29?lang=eng), [Alma 28:2](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/28?lang=eng), [3 Nephi 1:1–2](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/3-ne/1?lang=eng), and [3 Nephi 2:5–9](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/3-ne/2?lang=eng). Each explicitly recalls Lehi’s departure from Jerusalem; no actual carrier represents these contributions. Preserve 179 elapsed years, approximately 476 at accession, 509 at Mosiah’s death, the battle-severity comparison (not a numerical epoch), and the later 600/609-year notices. Distinguish age 30/63, 33 regnal years, judges-year clocks, the birth-sign clock and the records’ custody. No Enos age/prayer date, invented BCE conversion, filter year, new voyage or verified casualty census. Split into two readable cards if a single consolidation becomes crowded.
3. **RES96-C03, BMC-062, Jacob’s closing lament.** Enrich BC01 or an equivalent visible carrier with [Jacob 7:26](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/jacob/7?lang=eng). BC01 currently carries the Sherem dispute and plates transfer at 7:1–23,27, not the Jerusalem exile lament. Do not make Jacob born in Jerusalem, add a journey, or treat the life-as-dream simile as a literal vision.
4. **RES96-C04, BMC-098, records brought from Jerusalem.** [Alma 3:11–12](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/3?lang=eng) expressly identifies those records in the narrator’s account of community distinctions. The Sidon-only rationale misses it. Add a narrowly attributed source view/consolidation. Surrounding racialized mark/curse claims are not atlas judgments, modern ethnicity, archaeology/genetics, or Cain/Ham genealogy.
5. **RES96-C05, BMC-111, Jewish practice and prospective ministry.** [Alma 16:13,19–20](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/16?lang=eng) compares synagogue building with Jewish practice and teaches the Son’s future sufferings, death, resurrection and later appearance to this people. BXP10/SC04.6 do not carry this. Provide a source-only view or clear consolidation, preserving the future Book of Mormon appearance versus a witnessed Gospel scene. No modern synagogue, invented Bible site/person, or forced vine/grafting quotation from verse 17.
6. **RES96-C06, BMC-121, Israel-branch self-description.** [Alma 26:36–37](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/26?lang=eng) explicitly presents Ammon’s community as a branch of Israel in a strange land. The Ammon namesake decision is insufficient. Add an attributed collective-identity/metaphor view; no specified pedigree, modern ethnic mapping, literal branch route or required patriarch-person link.
7. **RES96-C07, BMC-008, Lehi’s dream.** [1 Nephi 8:2–38](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/1-ne/8?lang=eng) has no actual runtime carrier/direct source. BMV-001 is Lehi’s separate Jerusalem vision and BMV-011 is Nephi’s later vision. Add a source-only 3/bom account or actual consolidation and source-driven vision-content/mixed disposition. The old “vision diagram only” limit forbids surveyed geometry, not a source narrative. Optional 9:1 supports reporting context, not an engraving date. No dream river equals river Laman, forced Genesis/Revelation identity, mapped route or new absolute date.
8. **RES96-C08, BMC-103/133, distinct angelic accounts and recollection.** [Alma 8:14–21](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/8?lang=eng) describes an angel appearing to Alma and Amulek’s separate report of an angelic vision instructing hospitality. BXJ05/BXP08 carry route/redirection only. [Alma 38:7–8](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/38?lang=eng) recounts Alma’s earlier conversion encounter; BMV-131 and SC04.4 do not carry it. A coherent account can distinguish all three contributions and receive a source-driven disposition. 9. **RES96-C09, BMC-114, Lamoni, Abish and angel reports.** [Alma 19:12–16,29–34](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/19?lang=eng) reports Lamoni seeing his Redeemer, Abish’s earlier conversion associated with a “remarkable vision of her father,” and many reporting angels. SC04.5’s family copy does not represent these. Provide a source account/consolidation with source-driven classification, distinct attributions and the ambiguous father wording preserved. No invented father biography, Abish-as-queen identity, palace coordinate or claim Lamoni witnessed the later Gospel birth.
10. **RES96-C10, BMC-163, Helaman’s names and prison manifestation.** [Helaman 5:6,20–50](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/hel/5?lang=eng) recalls ancestors from Jerusalem and narrates fire without harm, shining faces, opened heavens and descending angels seen by about 300. BXJ10 describes the mission route, not these contributions. Enrich or add source-only carriers, optionally consolidating verse 6 with departure recollections; classify the manifestation from source. Keep observed appearances distinct from Aminadab’s explanation. No founding-brothers identity, biblical Aminadab, Daniel/Acts event identity or surveyed prison.
11. **RES96-C11, BMC-174, reported angels and skeptics’ Jerusalem argument.** [Helaman 16:13–14,18–20](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/hel/16?lang=eng) reports angel appearances to unnamed wise men, then separately quotes skeptics contrasting their land with distant Jerusalem. BXJ11’s Samuel route does not carry these. Add/consolidate a clearly attributed 3/bom source account and source-driven appearance disposition. Do not endorse the quoted skepticism, identify the wise men with Matthew 2’s visitors, or invent a journey/date/coordinate.
12. **RES96-C12, BMC-181, Nephi’s eyewitness angel account.** [3 Nephi 7:15,18](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/3-ne/7?lang=eng) explicitly says Nephi saw angels and describes their continuing ministry. BXE02’s collapse/continuing-ministry summary omits that. Enrich a carrier or add a narrow source account and source-driven disposition. Distinguish seeing angels, hearing the Lord’s voice and witnessing societal relapse; no automatic identification of his unnamed brother without 19:4. The dog/sow comparison to 2 Peter 2:22 is optional, not a claimed source mechanism.
13. **RES96-C13, BMC-193, witnessed ministry and light.** [3 Nephi 19:13–15,20–30,35–36](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/3-ne/19?lang=eng) supplies descending angels, Jesus’s return, prayer and luminous blessing not visible in BXR01/BXC02/BXE03. Enrich BXE03 with exact support or add a source account, then classify the actual carrier. Keep water unnamed, disciples distinct from Bible namesakes, and prayer to Jesus contextualized by his presence (v22). Attribute the Jews/faith comparison to Jesus rather than adopting a universal judgment about Jews. John 17 is an optional comparison, not the same prayer event.
14. **RES96-C14, BMC-223, Omer’s dream and Emer’s sight.** [Ether 9:3,21–22](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/ether/9?lang=eng) separately reports a warning dream causing Omer’s departure and Emer seeing the Son of Righteousness. BXJ14’s route and BXE06’s dynasty summary omit these. Enrich BXJ14’s causal copy or add a dream account, and separately represent Emer’s sight; classify both from source. No dream contents beyond the warning, described form/location of Emer’s sight, absolute dates, conflation of the two men, already-mortal/resurrected identity, or forced Numbers-serpent link.

Source accounts remain **3/bom**. Existing OTM-jerusalem (1/ot) or other supported targets are optional; target gates remain intact. Do not invent a Jesus person card. The accepted VIS-R1 principle is source-driven, not title-keyword-driven: use EXPLICIT membership when needed, preserve existing unrelated rows and both previously accepted source-only inclusions. A voice, general revelation notice, future conditional showing or undescribed visitation must not automatically become a visual scene.

### Explicit dispositions for the other 76 chapter keys

These are full-read editorial decisions, not certified absence of every shared phrase. Carry the precise grounds into source-view reasons/rollups rather than relying on multi-chapter range spill. No separate new Bible card is compulsory for these keys; preserve existing visible coverage and the safeguards below.

- **BMC-009**, [1 Nephi 9](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/1-ne/9?lang=eng): Two-plate purposes; 9:1 recalls Lehi’s reporting context. No new Bible episode, duplicate vision or engraving date.
- **BMC-018**, [1 Nephi 18](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/1-ne/18?lang=eng): Repair the reason: Jerusalem seed provenance at 18:24 is represented by BE04/BJ01. No separate voyage; preserve namesakes.
- **BMC-054**, [2 Nephi 32](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/2-ne/32?lang=eng): Christ doctrine, prayer and future manifestation. Matthew 7:7–8/Luke 18:1 are optional comparisons, not identified Gospel events; replace Zion range spill.
- **BMC-055**, [2 Nephi 33](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/2-ne/33?lang=eng): Jews/Israel and the words of Jews in a closing address. Narrow-gate comparison is optional; no visit to Zion or literal vision.
- **BMC-058**, [Jacob 3](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/jacob/3?lang=eng): Fidelity and anti-reviling instruction; David/Solomon belong to chapter 2. Preserve attribution and do not make ethnic judgments.
- **BMC-064**, [Jarom 1](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/jarom/1?lang=eng): Law of Moses/Sabbath at 1:5 and future Messiah at 1:11; retain BC03/SC04.2. General revelations are not separately narrated visions.
- **BMC-066**, [Words of Mormon 1](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/w-of-m/1?lang=eng): BC04 represents Mormon’s editorial record transfer. Benjamin’s prior reign remains separate; false Christs and Laban do not identify Gospel/Genesis people.
- **BMC-074**, [Mosiah 8](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/8?lang=eng): Explicitly address 8:8’s hosts-of-Israel numerical simile, not ancestry. General seer/interpreter teaching is not a new vision scene; no forced edge.
- **BMC-075**, [Mosiah 9](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/9?lang=eng): BXJ03 covers Zeniff’s expeditions. Unnamed fathers’ deliverance in 9:17 is not an identified Exodus; keep hostile descriptions attributed.
- **BMC-077**, [Mosiah 11](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/11?lang=eng): Noah is Zeniff’s son; towers/temple do not identify Babel/Solomon. Retain BXP03 and practice/narrative decision.
- **BMC-083**, [Mosiah 17](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/17?lang=eng): Book of Mormon king Noah and Abinadi’s execution; BC05 covers Alma’s records/escape. Attribute accusations; no identified Gospel trial.
- **BMC-085**, [Mosiah 19](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/19?lang=eng): Local Noah/Gideon; distinguish fleeing men, families and priests. No biblical judge identity or merged flight.
- **BMC-086**, [Mosiah 20](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/20?lang=eng): Judges 21:19–23 is a permissible abduction/dance comparison, not the same event or consent. No mandatory Bible link.
- **BMC-087**, [Mosiah 21](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/21?lang=eng): Ammon’s search and physical ruins/dry bones are not Ezekiel’s vision. Baptism is deferred; no duplicate earlier search.
- **BMC-088**, [Mosiah 22](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/22?lang=eng): BE09/BJ04/BXJ04 represent Limhi’s escape. Bondage/flight alone is not Egypt or Moses; preserve local namesakes.
- **BMC-089**, [Mosiah 23](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/mosiah/23?lang=eng): Repair Noah-only reason: 23:23 invokes the God of Abraham/Isaac/Jacob, not their visit or pedigree. Galatians 5:1 comparison remains optional.

- **BMC-096**, [Alma 1](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/1?lang=eng): Gideon is the local teacher. Attribute Nehor’s teaching; general charity/priestly practice does not require a Gospel scene.
- **BMC-097**, [Alma 2](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/2?lang=eng): Local river Sidon and valley named for the slain Gideon; BXP06/07/34 cover geography. No Phoenician city or Bible judge.
- **BMC-099**, [Alma 4](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/4?lang=eng): Record prospective Christ/resurrection belief at 4:13–14, rather than only institutions. No witnessed Gospel event or new Jesus person card.
- **BMC-101**, [Alma 6](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/6?lang=eng): Aaron is absent from this chapter: remove range spill. BXJ05 covers Gideon/Sidon route; 6:8 is future teaching.
- **BMC-109**, [Alma 14](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/alma/14?lang=eng): Acts 16 prison comparison is optional with different outcomes/victims. No one earthquake or named Bible book inferred from burned holy records.

- **BMC-167**, [Helaman 9](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/hel/9?lang=eng): Murder investigation, confession and release remain distinct. Divine knowledge does not describe sight; popular claims that Nephi is a god are attributed.
- **BMC-168**, [Helaman 10](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/hel/10?lang=eng): Acknowledge audible commission/transport, not visual God/angels. Matthew 16:19 comparison is optional; no Peter identity, home interior or completed hypothetical destruction.

- **BMC-206**, [Mormon 1](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/morm/1?lang=eng): Acknowledge Mormon’s undescribed visitation at 1:15, not textual absence. No automatic visual scene/date; retain Sidon and planned/completed retrieval distinctions.
- **BMC-207**, [Mormon 2](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/morm/2?lang=eng): Repair exact chapter names: David/Joshua lands, king Aaron, Samuel the Lamanite and Shem. Boaz/Jordan are later range spill; do not export kingship to Moroni 9.
- **BMC-209**, [Mormon 4](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/morm/4?lang=eng): Explicitly acknowledge 4:12’s broad house-of-Israel wickedness comparison and decline a separate person/site edge. Keep attribution, victims and retrieval stages.
- **BMC-211**, [Mormon 6](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/morm/6?lang=eng): Correct Gilgal to commander at 6:14; BXR03 already gets it right. Ramah comes from separate Ether 15 alias support; no forced Pauline resurrection scene.
- **BMC-219**, [Ether 5](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/ether/5?lang=eng): BXC04 covers future witnesses/sealed matter. Deuteronomy 19 comparison is optional; prospective showing is not completed vision or named modern witnesses.
- **BMC-221**, [Ether 7](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/ether/7?lang=eng): Local Jaredite Noah, Nimrod and hill Ephraim; existing geography/family/dynasty cards suffice. Distinguish the Cohors; no biblical Exodus/metallurgy proof.
- **BMC-224**, [Ether 10](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/ether/10?lang=eng): Repair Aaron to captive at 10:31, not an unsupported king. Keep exact Jaredite roles and son/descendant distinctions; no Levite/King Saul/Moses identity.
- **BMC-225**, [Ether 11](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/ether/11?lang=eng): Jaredite captive Seth and existing dynasty/genealogy coverage. Preserve Shiblom/Shiblon variants and the rejected 5/pgp target; no unnamed-usurper identity.
- **BMC-228**, [Ether 14](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/ether/14?lang=eng): Gilead is Shared’s brother; Jared/Noah/Nimrod are range spill. Preserve separate armies and later Lib; collapse is not Coriantumr’s death.
- **BMC-229**, [Ether 15](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/ether/15?lang=eng): Existing terminal-campaign/Ramah-Cumorah coverage suffices. Ether’s command is auditory and fate unresolved; no Jeremiah, New York or narrated translation.
- **BMC-230**, [Moroni 1](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/moro/1?lang=eng): BC07/BXJ17/BXE08 cover survival, unknown wandering and writing. Allegiance to Christ is not a separate Gospel scene; no grave/date of death.
- **BMC-231**, [Moroni 2](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/moro/2?lang=eng): BC08 covers retrospective instruction at the first appearance, not a new Moroni-era manifestation. Acts 8 comparison is optional; no apostle identities.
- **BMC-232**, [Moroni 3](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/moro/3?lang=eng): BC09 covers ordination practice. No named ordinands, temple venue, date or separate Bible ceremony is supplied.
- **BMC-233**, [Moroni 4](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/moro/4?lang=eng): BC10 covers bread prayer. 1 Corinthians 11 comparison is optional, not a new Jerusalem supper or claimed quotation source.
- **BMC-234**, [Moroni 5](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/moro/5?lang=eng): BC10 covers wine prayer. Preserve wine; modern water practice needs another source. No extra event, venue or journey.
- **BMC-235**, [Moroni 6](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/moro/6?lang=eng): BC11/BXC06 cover baptism, care, meetings and discipline. Hebrews 12/Deuteronomy 19 comparisons are optional; disciplinary witnesses differ from Ether 5’s plate witnesses.

### Separate bounded closure of the two 3 Nephi 11 Matthew reader leads

At PR33 checkpoint 61 (`4ea873875f73746a754bd537deaaebb1a58d586b`), both remaining reader leads were independently compared against 28 selected official primary verses: [3 Nephi 11:31–41](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/bofm/3-ne/11?lang=eng), [Matthew 16:13–20](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/nt/matt/16?lang=eng), and [Matthew 7:21–29](https://www.churchofjesuschrist.org/study/scriptures/nt/matt/7?lang=eng). Both are substantive textual/image comparisons; neither establishes the same event, audience or literary dependence. This is separate from the residual-96 batch and does not alter its source scope or counts.

**MAT61-C1: add one source-only comparison card, 3/bom, with no `see` person/event target.** Suggested stable key `BMV-185-doctrine-foundations-matthew`; title **“Rock and sand compared with Matthew (3 Nephi 11)”**. Suggested copy:

Book of Mormon — Jesus describes his doctrine in terms of faith, repentance, baptism, and witness from the Father, Son and Holy Ghost. He calls building on that doctrine building on his rock; declaring more or less and establishing it as his doctrine is a sandy foundation. Compare Matthew 16:18’s rock/church/gates saying and Matthew 7:24–27’s storm-tested houses. Matthew 16 addresses Peter; Matthew 7 contrasts hearing and doing Jesus’s words with hearing without doing them. These are comparisons of wording and images, not one setting, audience or event.

Use source locators **3 Nephi 11:31–40; Matthew 16:13–20; Matthew 7:21–29**, each with its official URL above. Keep the condition “and establishing it as his doctrine”; do not imply every statement beyond this passage is condemned. Preserve the different applications: Third Nephi’s doctrine/builders, Matthew 16’s address to Peter and church building, Matthew 7’s hearing-and-doing. Do not arbitrate Matthew’s rock referent, add Peter to Bountiful, identify a historical flood, create a timed/spatial scene, or assert that Third Nephi names Matthew as its source.

Leave `BMV-185-crucified-christ-wounds-bountiful` at 11:11–15 unchanged. Also preserve BMV-188’s separate 3 Nephi 14 / Matthew 7:1–14 judgment-and-asking comparison; it does not already cover these later verse ranges.

Record the new source-view decision and change exactly the two BMC-185 `reader` entries in `bmc_textscan_triage.py` to `resolved`, referring to this one comparison view: cue names `parallel: Matthew 16:18` and `parallel: Matthew 7:26-27` stay unchanged. Regenerate the CSV without expanding the frozen 39-chapter / 114-row screen. Holding other rows fixed, expected triage becomes **91 consistent, 7 no-hit, 16 resolved, 0 reader**. This closes these two screen leads only, not the residual-96 implementation or a full semantic/vision/release gate.

Return the exact head and diff, verify all other cue identities/locators/states and Cain behavior unchanged, preserve existing cards/targets/gates, and test the new card’s 3/bom visibility, search, source links and deep links. No new year, map pin, vision membership or Bible identity link is needed for this teaching card. No approval, merge, deployment, review-artifact publication or status/lock change. Keep this as a separately labeled unit; it may be implemented alongside the coherent atlas batch without changing that batch’s 96-key accounting.

## 2026-11-09 — Simulator clarity and physics implementation handoff

This handoff queues the prepared simulator work for Claude. It is not evidence of builder pickup, implementation, review acceptance or release.

Stage: research-ready. Owner: Claude. Next action: implement the first bounded slice below on a draft PR, after reconciling any already claimed work, then return its exact head for independent review. This entry does not change any other item's status or release decision.

**Requested outcome:** remove the distracting geometric ignition cage; make the simulator's gas motion and close black-hole encounters visibly plausible; improve graphics and frame-time reporting; simplify mobile labels and routine event feedback; add a child-friendly mode; provide black-hole size presets and named examples with honest units.

**Implementation owner:** Claude. **Independent return review:** ChatGPT. Keep changes on a draft implementation PR. Preserve current owner release gates. Do not merge/deploy based on this handoff. Do not overwrite PR44 or re-run completed unrelated research.

### Current verified state

On 2026-11-08, main e68d411b71eb38178ce0ee2235e3d245c715dc8f had the same complete src tree (24c3f502f19d5958da61de8fcc8c7980db61653d) as the latest verified Pages deployment, 6b65a79cd28a1f6fd8ed278b2779010a72fb90bc. Deploy run 42438026686 succeeded at 2026-11-08T02:26:03Z. The new requests below were absent from that source. At the 2026-11-09 check of main 42f685e262523cca923ea0575ab4ab59f23b7a26, effects.ts, disk-flow.ts, loop.ts and celestial-catalog.ts still match their reviewed source blobs. PR44 remains open at the head below. Recheck current source before implementation; this dated record does not assert an unpublished branch is empty.

PR44 at 2e305cc24ad714be8211d839d283d114748a4310 is an older, separately reviewed mobile touch/layout change and remains open, awaiting the D06 release decision. It does not implement the new label/quiet/Kids scope. The older PR43 disk/accretion remediation is live; do not mistake it for the new binary-disk request.

### First returned implementation slice

Start with items 1–3 together, then continue the mobile and catalog units without reopening completed source research. Read the latest branch before editing.

1. **Ignition cage:** src/visuals/effects.ts stellarIgnitionEffect creates three IcosahedronGeometry shells, with the third wireframe enabled at line 415. Remove the geometric cage, retain legible restrained fusion feedback and distinguish fusion beginning from a supernova. The screenshot strongly matches this effect, but its blurred label cannot identify whether the triggering object was a protostar or brown dwarf. The shell is visual-only; do not add physical shockwave forces to explain an artistic effect.
2. **Disk shader correctness:** implement/reconcile the prepared two-file correction in disk-flow.ts and materials.ts: interpolate disk XY and compute atan in the fragment shader, rather than interpolating the discontinuous polar angle; replace the three reversed smoothstep disk masks with 1-smoothstep(low,high,x). The candidate preserves the flow model. It does not claim to fix binary fluid response.
3. **Timing and other shader masks:** sample elapsed frame time once; use uncapped finite foreground time for FPS/adaptive-quality measurement and the separate safe capped step for physics. Reset measurement windows at hide/show. Fix the gas-vortex and jet reversed-edge smoothstep calls. Preserve manual quality modes and fixed-step safety.

### Subsequent bounded units

4. **Disk readability and binary response:** current shaders and 52/108 inflow points animate, but local disk motion has no companion input. Visual time caps at 4x and outer-edge shader periods span 25–186 visual seconds. Keep the broad view-dependent Doppler highlight fixed relative to the camera while trackable material features orbit/shear. Introduce explicit bounded encounter state using companion position, mass ratio and separation, preserving phase and disk plane. Let outer material truncate/perturb and selected tracers transfer or escape as encounters strengthen; distant disks should remain stable. Treat this as a labeled approximation, not GRMHD. Merge transitions must retire old disks cleanly and must not imply gas-free black holes necessarily make a visible explosion. Coordinate physical units with item 7.
5. **Graphics:** replace fixed local-axis planetary lighting with a consistent star-relative direction and a documented no-star fallback. Keep body spin, camera motion and source motion distinct. Use restrained atmosphere/cloud/specular effects; return matched before/after scenes and actual performance numbers rather than a different renderer or concept image.
6. **Mobile, quiet feedback and Kids mode:** compact default shows at most the selected object's short label; move metrics to deliberate Details, reset the compact sheet on fresh selection, preserve accessible full names. Separate event-history storage from presentation; suppress unsolicited simulation banners/live announcements in quiet/Kids mode without hiding action failures. Kids controls are Add, Pause/Play, Undo and Home, with Explore tap/select and drag/pan, exactly one placement per armed Add, explicit reversible grown-up controls and pointer/keyboard/context-menu gating. Do not reset scenes or idle-replace a child's scene. Preserve save/load, history and adult preferences. Test short landscape, 200% text, interruption/cancel/repeated taps, keyboard and screen-reader semantics.
7. **Black-hole catalog and sizes:** five friendly presets are not five scientific classes. Proposed masses: 5, 30, 1,000, 1 million and 1 billion solar masses. Use the named examples and uncertainty labels below. Schwarzschild-equivalent radius is 2GM/c², about 2.95325 km per solar mass; distinguish horizon, shadow, accretion-disk extent and display/picking radius. Do not insert true solar masses into the current arbitrary-unit solver, velocity clamp or display-derived contacts. Design a common unit mapping or honestly label legacy model units first. Do not claim a Newtonian/softened toy model is accurate near a horizon.

### Required return and acceptance

Return one exact branch/head per bounded unit, changed files, passed/failed/not-run checks, known gaps and the next concrete action. Run repository format/check/build/tests on the integrated final source. Include real shader compilation and desktop/compact browser interaction, console checks and matched clips for isolated/wide/close/equal/unequal/flyby/merger disks. Physical iPhone/Safari and real-GPU testing must remain explicitly unverified unless performed.

Preparatory local evidence: 10 disk/ignition source-controller-math test groups, 50 graphics source/controller/math checks, 18 mobile source-evidence checks with 32 pending runtime acceptance cases, and 21 catalog/reference-model checks. These counts do not represent a production integration or rendered acceptance pass.

Primary science references:
- https://svs.gsfc.nasa.gov/13326/ — differential rotation, shearing structure and Doppler contrast.
- https://www.nasa.gov/universe/new-nasa-visualization-probes-the-light-bending-dance-of-binary-black-holes/ — material motion versus lensed images.
- https://arxiv.org/abs/1310.0492 and https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.01307 — minidisks, streams and regime-dependent gas behavior.
- https://science.nasa.gov/universe/stars/ — fusion onset and stellar lifecycle.
- https://github.com/KhronosGroup/OpenGL-Refpages/blob/main/gl4/smoothstep.xml — ordered smoothstep edges.


### Self-contained implementation details

The original working files were prepared outside this repository. This section carries the actionable content so Claude does not need access to another machine's local paths. Do not claim those local tests as newly executed repository tests.

For the disk correction:
- In DISK_VERTEX, replace the interpolated angle varying with a vec2 local-position varying, assign position.xy to it, and compute the orbital direction for Doppler beaming from a local vertex angle as before.
- Update all three consumer fragment interfaces in materials.ts together. Pass atan(localPosition.y, localPosition.x) into diskFlow. Preserve the two-phase flow equations and inner-fast/outer-slow behavior.
- Black-hole outer mask: smoothstep(1., .68, r) becomes 1. - smoothstep(.68, 1., r).
- Protostar outer mask: smoothstep(1., .72, r) becomes 1. - smoothstep(.72, 1., r).
- Transfer outer mask: smoothstep(1., .8, r) becomes 1. - smoothstep(.8, 1., r).
- Separate graphics masks: gas vortex smoothstep(.45, .12, oval) becomes 1. - smoothstep(.12, .45, oval); jet smoothstep(1.28, .12, vEdge) becomes 1. - smoothstep(.12, 1.28, vEdge).
- Regression cases must include interpolation across both sides of the -pi/+pi seam, matching varying declarations for all three disks, finite monotonic taper values, pause and speeds above 4x, and persistent isolated-disk motion. These tests do not substitute for GPU shader compilation.

For honest timing, the measured defect is that a real 0.1-second frame is clamped to 0.03 before the monitor sees it, so it reports about 33 FPS rather than 10. The same error stretches a nominal 2.5-second adaptive-quality window to about 8.4 wall seconds. Preserve a separate bounded simulation delta and test background/resume behavior rather than removing all timestep safety.

For mobile and Kids mode, keep every behavior testable at its event handler, not just hidden by CSS. Preserve errors, save/load and construction history. Home means fit the existing scene, not reset it. A quiet mode must not replace banners with a pulsing counter or replay suppressed messages on exit. Do not infer a real device pass from an emulated viewport.

### Black-hole catalog reference values

Use rounded, cited reference masses with uncertainty/status labels. Derived sizes are Schwarzschild-equivalent horizons for the stated nonspinning model, not measured shadow or disk diameters. Presets are UI sizes; the two largest are both scientifically supermassive. A mass change must scale the model horizon linearly, while display exaggeration and picking remain separate.

- **Cygnus X-1:** approximately 21 solar masses. Rounded NASA value, not an exact measured mass. Accreting X-ray binary; no measured disk outer size supplied. Source: https://www.nasa.gov/universe/nasas-ixpe-reveals-shape-orientation-of-hot-matter-around-black-hole/
- **Gaia BH3:** approximately 33 solar masses. Rounded ESA reference value, not an exact measured mass; observed estimates have uncertainty. Dormant: do not give it a bright disk just because it is massive. Source: https://www.esa.int/ESA_Multimedia/Images/2024/04/Gaia_black_holes
- **GW190521 remnant:** approximately 142 solar masses. Original 2020 quasi-circular binary inference, source-frame median; 90% credible interval +28/-16 solar masses. Model-dependent, not a nearby imaged object. Do not invent a measured disk or jet. Source: https://dcc.ligo.org/public/0165/P2000021/012/gw190521-implications-main_20200901.pdf
- **HLX-1:** approximately 20,000 solar masses. Representative NASA estimate; uncertain, not a confirmed exact mass. Candidate accreting object; disk rendering illustrative. Source: https://science.nasa.gov/missions/hubble/nasas-hubble-spots-a-relic-from-a-shredded-galaxy/
- **Sagittarius A*:** approximately 4,000,000 solar masses. Rounded NASA reference value; precision dynamical estimates differ. Low accretion activity; use illustrative image morphology, not a universal bright disk. Source: https://science.nasa.gov/universe/black-holes/types/
- **M87*:** approximately 6,500,000,000 solar masses. Rounded EHT/NASA mass estimate; uncertainty is not zero. Accretion and jet context; do not infer disk diameter from mass alone. Source: https://www.jpl.nasa.gov/edu/resources/teachable-moment/how-scientists-captured-the-first-image-of-a-black-hole/

Use one documented scene-wide conversion for the gravitational parameter, such as mu_sim = mu_SI * T0^2 / L0^3. Do not silently assign true solar masses to legacy model masses printed as Earth masses, reuse the current cube-root body-growth law for a physical horizon, or derive collision thresholds from a selection hit target.

After implementation, return passed, failed and unavailable checks separately. ChatGPT will review the exact returned revision; Mckay's release gate remains separate.
